\displaystyle+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}},H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{2}^{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{2}},2H^{\text{LO}}_{(\text{ES}_{1}^{2})(\text{ES}_{2}^{2})}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},2H^{\text{LO}}_{(\text{ES}_{1}^{2})(\text{ES}_{2}^{2})}\}_{S}.

We then write the solution of
\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}
as:

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}\frac{\vec{x}_{1}+\vec{x}_{2}}{2}+\vec{Y}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}},

(57)

with:

\displaystyle\vec{Y}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{GC_{1\text{ES}^{4}}m_{2}}{4m_{1}{}^{3}r{}^{4}}\Big[3\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{1}-5(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{3}\vec{S}_{1}\Big]-\frac{GC_{1\text{BS}^{3}}m_{2}}{2m_{1}{}^{3}r{}^{4}}\Big[5S_{1}^{2}\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}-\vec{n}S_{1}^{4}

(58)

\displaystyle+\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{1}-5(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{3}\vec{S}_{1}\Big]+\frac{3GC_{1\text{ES}^{2}}m_{2}}{4m_{1}{}^{3}r{}^{4}}\Big[5S_{1}^{2}\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}-\vec{n}S_{1}^{4}+\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{1}

\displaystyle-5(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{3}\vec{S}_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle-\frac{3G}{2m_{1}{}^{2}r{}^{4}}\Big[S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{n}-5\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{2}\vec{n}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}-2\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{S}_{1}+5\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}\vec{S}_{1}

\displaystyle+\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{2}\Big]-\frac{GC_{1\text{BS}^{3}}}{4m_{1}{}^{2}r{}^{4}}\Big[2S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{n}-10\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}-S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}

\displaystyle+2\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{S}_{1}+5\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}\vec{S}_{1}+3\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{2}-5(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{3}\vec{S}_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{3GC_{1\text{ES}^{2}}}{2m_{1}{}^{2}r{}^{4}}\Big[S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{n}-5\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+2\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{S}_{1}-3\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{2}

\displaystyle+5(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{3}\vec{S}_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle-\frac{3GC_{1\text{ES}^{2}}C_{2\text{ES}^{2}}}{4m_{1}m_{2}r{}^{4}}\Big[5S_{1}^{2}\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+2\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{S}_{1}+3\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{2}^{2}\vec{S}_{1}

\displaystyle-5\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}\vec{S}_{1}\Big]-\frac{3GC_{1\text{ES}^{2}}}{4m_{1}m_{2}r{}^{4}}\Big[S_{1}^{2}S_{2}^{2}\vec{n}-5S_{2}^{2}\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+2\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{2}^{2}\vec{S}_{1}

\displaystyle-S_{1}^{2}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{2}-2\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}\vec{S}_{2}+5\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}\vec{S}_{2}\Big]+\left(1\leftrightarrow 2\right).

Thus we found a solution for the Poincaré algebra of the NLO quartic-in-spin sectors, with our new
general Hamiltonian from eq. (
20
), and this provides significant confidence in the
validity of these new results.

4.2
N
3
LO Quadratic-in-Spin Sectors

To confirm the new precision frontier at the
5
PN order across all sectors, we proceed to solve for
the Poincaré algebra of the N
3
LO quadratic-in-spin sectors.
Again, we start by decomposing
H
and
\vec{G}
to all the sectors relevant to the solution for

\vec{G}
at the N
3
LO
quadratic-in-spin sectors in question. The Hamiltonian consists of:

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H_{\text{N}}+H_{1\text{PN}}+H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}+H_{2\text{PN}}+H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}+H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{SO}}+H_{3\text{PN}}+H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

(59)

\displaystyle+H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}+H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}+H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}+H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}},

with
H^{\text{N${}^{3}$LO}}_{\text{SO}}
taken from our
Kim:2022pou
, and

H^{\text{N${}^{3}$LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}
from our
Kim:2022bwv
, and we have:

\displaystyle H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),\qquad H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{SO}}=H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(60)

\displaystyle H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),\qquad H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}=H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(61)

\displaystyle H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}+H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(62)

\displaystyle H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}+H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(63)

\displaystyle H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}+H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(64)

\displaystyle H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{2}}H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{E}^{2}\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2).

(65)

For the COM generator we have:

\displaystyle\vec{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{G}_{\text{N}}+\vec{G}_{1\text{PN}}+\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}+\vec{G}_{2\text{PN}}+\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{SO}}+\vec{G}_{3\text{PN}}+\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}+\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}+\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

(66)

\displaystyle+\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}+\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}},

where we used again that all generators of LO sectors with spin vanish beyond spin-orbit, and with:

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),\qquad\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{SO}}=\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(67)

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),\quad\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}=\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(68)

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}+\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(69)

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}+\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(70)

where we solved for
\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{SO}}
in
Levi:2022dqm
,
and now we should solve for:

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{2}}\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{E}^{2}\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2).

(71)

Let us then list the set of decoupled equations to solve for the generators of each sector.
We solve for
\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}
from:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}},H_{\text{N}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}_{\text{N}},H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}_{1\text{PN}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}\}_{x}

(72)

\displaystyle+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{2\text{PN}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{2\text{PN}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{x}

\displaystyle+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}},H_{1\text{PN}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{S}

\displaystyle+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{S}.

We solve for
\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}
from:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}},H_{\text{N}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}_{\text{N}},H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}_{1\text{PN}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{2\text{PN}}\}_{x}

(73)

\displaystyle+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{2\text{PN}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}},H_{1\text{PN}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{3\text{PN}},H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}\}_{S}

\displaystyle+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}},H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{S}.\qquad\qquad

We solve for
\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{E}^{2}\text{S}_{1}^{2}}
from:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{E}^{2}\text{S}_{1}^{2}},H_{\text{N}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}_{\text{N}},H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{E}^{2}\text{S}_{1}^{2}}\}_{x}.

(74)

We solve for
\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}
from:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}},H_{\text{N}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}_{\text{N}},H^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{2}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}_{1\text{PN}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}\}_{x}

(75)

\displaystyle+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{2}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}},H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{2\text{PN}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{2\text{PN}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{2}},H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}}\}_{x}

\displaystyle+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}},H_{1\text{PN}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{PN}},H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}\}_{x}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{2}},2H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{2}},2H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}\}_{S}

\displaystyle+\{2\vec{G}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}},H^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{2}}\}_{S}+\{\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{2}},2H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}\}_{S}+\{2\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}},H^{\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{2}}\}_{S}+\left(1\leftrightarrow 2\right).

We then write the solution of
\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}
as:

\displaystyle\vec{G}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{\text{N}^{2}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}^{2}}\frac{\vec{x}_{1}+\vec{x}_{2}}{2}+\left(\vec{Y}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\vec{Y}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{2}}+\vec{Y}^{\text{N}^{3}\text{LO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+\left(1\leftrightarrow 2\right)\right),

(76)

where we present the explicit expressions in appendix
D
.

Thus, we solved for the Poincaré algebra of the N
3
LO quadratic-in-spin sectors,
with our full general Hamiltonians first presented in
Kim:2022bwv
, and this provides
significant confidence in the validity of these results.
It should be highlighted that though an agreement with the consequent observables in specific
restricted configurations, which we first provided in
Kim:2021rfj
, was found later in

Mandal:2022ufb
, the Poincaré algebra which we solved for here, provides the most stringent
check on the most comprehensive Hamiltonian results.
This then completes the Poincaré algebra across all sectors with spins at the new precision
frontier at the
5
PN order.

5
Specialized Hamiltonians and Observables

Obtaining Hamiltonians is essential to derive EOMs, EOB models, or as we have shown, to study the global
Poincaré algebra of the binary system. However, Hamiltonians are also critical to get sensible binding
energies, which are key to GW applications. To that end, we gradually simplify our
general Hamiltonians from section
3.2
, by considering specific kinematic configurations, and
eventually removing all gauge-dependence to get physical observables.
First, we define some binary-mass conventions:

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle\equiv m_{1}+m_{2},\quad q\equiv m_{1}/m_{2},

(77)

\displaystyle\mu\equiv m_{1}m_{2}/m,\quad\nu

\displaystyle\equiv m_{1}m_{2}/m^{2}=\mu/m=q/(1+q)^{2},

(78)

where
q
and
\nu
are the dimensionless mass ratio and symmetric mass-ratio, respectively, that are
used to express the final observables. All the variables are also converted to dimensionless ones,
denoted with a tilde, where
Gm
and
\mu
are the length and mass units, respectively.
The general Hamiltonians are then specified to the COM frame, reducing to a single momentum vector,

\vec{p}\equiv\vec{p}_{1}=-\vec{p}_{2}
, with which the orbital angular momentum is defined,

\vec{L}\equiv r\vec{n}\times\vec{p}
.

The NLO quartic-in-spin Hamiltonians in the COM frame are then:

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{(\text{ES}_{1}^{2})\text{S}_{1}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{(\text{ES}_{1}^{2})^{2}}+C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{(\text{BS}_{1}^{3})\text{S}_{1}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{ES}_{1}^{4}}+C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{E}^{2}\text{S}_{1}^{4}}

(79)

\displaystyle+\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{3}\text{S}_{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{(\text{ES}_{1}^{2})\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{C_{\text{ES}^{2}_{1}}^{2}\text{S}_{1}^{3}\text{S}_{2}}+C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{(\text{BS}_{1}^{3})\text{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle+\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}\text{S}_{2}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{(\text{ES}_{1}^{2})\text{S}_{2}^{2}}+C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}C_{2\text{ES}^{2}}\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{(\text{ES}_{1}^{2})(\text{ES}_{2}^{2})}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

where we present the explicit expressions in appendix
E
.
Very few scattering-amplitudes approaches to the scattering problem also present
analogous Hamiltonians for the bound problem, though only for low loop orders, as explained in
section
1
and illustrated in table
1
, due to radiation-reaction effects that
kick in as of the N
2
LO. In any case, those analogous Hamiltonians are already restricted to the COM
frame
Chen:2021qkk
;
Bern:2022kto
.

As noted in
Levi:2022dqm
,
Chen:2021qkk
and
Bern:2022kto
differ between them
in several ways already at the level of their physical scattering amplitudes, which underlie their
subsequent COM Hamiltonians for BHs, and for generic compact objects, respectively.
The results in
Chen:2021qkk
have been known to be discrepant with ours as of our verified
cubic-in-spin orders, which was already clarified in
Levi:2022dqm
, and at the present
quartic-in-spin sectors.

As was also clarified in
Levi:2022dqm
, the COM Hamiltonians presented in
Bern:2022kto

for generic compact binaries, have been known to be discrepant even for BHs, with our results as of
the LO cubic-in-spin sectors in
Levi:2014gsa
, which have been well-verified along the years.
This discrepancy is due to singularities that show up as of cubic order in spin on
Bern:2022kto
.
At the NLO the COM Hamiltonians in
Bern:2022kto
for the quartic-in-spin sectors exhibit a
growing discrepancy, including similar singularities even for BHs.
Moreover, at the NLO quartic-in-spin sectors the work in
Bern:2022kto
contained contributions
with
3
claimed new Wilson coefficients,
H_{2}
,
H_{3}
, and
H_{4}
, which they stipulated in their
formulation. Such extra free parameters violate spin-gauge invariance
Levi:2015msa
, and are
also absent and discrepant with other corresponding physical scattering amplitudes, e.g. in

Chen:2021qkk
.
Moreover, there was an inconsistent omission in
Bern:2022kto
of the new subleading spin-induced
hexadecapular deformation operator, as noted in section
2
, and thus the consequent
contribution is also missing in their results.

A further simplification is obtained by constraining the spins to be aligned with the orbital angular
momentum, thus also requiring
\vec{S}_{a}\cdot\vec{n}=\vec{S}_{a}\cdot\vec{p}=0
.
However, this simplification is notably inappropriate at higher-spin sectors, as it entails a growing
loss of information on the physics of the system – the higher in spin the sectors are – as of quadratic order in
the spin. For the present quartic-in-spin sectors, the aligned-spins simplification leads to the
following total Hamiltonian:

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu^{2}\tilde{S}_{1}^{4}}{\tilde{r}^{6}}\left[C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}\left(\frac{3}{2}-\frac{3\nu}{2}-\frac{3\nu}{4}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{45}{16}-\frac{21\nu}{8}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(-\frac{3\nu^{2}}{2}+\frac{9\nu}{2}-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{4}-\frac{3\nu^{2}}{2}\right)+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(-\frac{39\nu^{2}}{16}+\frac{33\nu}{4}-\frac{45}{16}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}^{2}\left(-\frac{\nu}{8}+\frac{1}{q}\left(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\nu}{8}\right)\right)+C_{1(\text{BS}^{3})}\left(-\frac{\nu}{2}-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{3\nu}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\right)+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(-\frac{\nu^{2}}{2}-\frac{5\nu}{2}+\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu^{2}}{2}-\frac{9\nu}{2}+\frac{3}{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{3\nu^{2}}{2}-\frac{9\nu}{2}+\frac{3}{2}\right)\right)\right)+C_{1(\text{ES}^{4})}\left(\frac{5\nu}{2}-\frac{23}{8}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{16}+\frac{9}{16}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{9\nu}{8}+\frac{9}{16}\right)+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\frac{5\nu^{2}}{2}-\frac{33\nu}{4}+\frac{23}{8}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu^{2}}{8}+\frac{15\nu}{16}-\frac{9}{16}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{9\nu^{2}}{4}-\frac{9}{16}\right)\right)\right)+\frac{27}{35}C_{1(\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4})}\left(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\nu}{8}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(-\frac{\nu^{2}}{8}+\frac{3\nu}{8}-\frac{1}{8}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu^{3}\tilde{S}_{1}^{3}\tilde{S}_{2}}{\tilde{r}^{6}}\left[3+\frac{45}{8}\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(3\nu-3+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{2}\right)+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{21\nu}{4}-\frac{45}{8}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle+C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}\left(\frac{5}{2}-\frac{9}{2}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{9}{2}\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\frac{5\nu}{2}+6+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{9}{2}-6\nu\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{9}{2}-\frac{33\nu}{8}\right)\right)\right)+C_{1(\text{BS}^{3})}\left(-6+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}+\frac{3}{2}\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\frac{23}{2}-6\nu+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{4}-\frac{13}{4}\right)+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{7}{4}-3\nu\right)\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu^{2}\tilde{S}_{1}^{2}\tilde{S}_{2}^{2}}{2\tilde{r}^{6}}\left[3-3\nu\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}+\frac{3\nu}{4}\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}+2C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}\left(4\nu+3+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{9}{2}-\frac{15\nu}{4}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(\frac{27}{16}-\frac{15\nu}{8}\right)+\frac{\nu}{q}\left(4-\frac{9}{2}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}-\frac{27}{16}\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left.+C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}C_{2(\text{ES}^{2})}\left(9+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(-\frac{9\nu}{8}-\frac{33}{8}\right)+\tilde{p}_{r}^{2}\tilde{r}\left(-\frac{27\nu}{4}-\frac{3}{8}\right)\right)\right]+(1\leftrightarrow 2).

Due to the aforementioned loss of information in the aligned-spins configuration, we see that similar
to what happens at the NLO cubic-in-sectors, the unique sector in the potentials and in
eq. (
E
), that is proportional to both
C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}
and
S_{2}
, vanishes here, and
thus it is absent in all the common observables that assume this simplified restriction.

Finally, in the long quasi-circular inspiral phase, it is reasonable to assume the circular-orbit
condition,
p_{r}\equiv\vec{p}\cdot\vec{n}=0\Rightarrow p^{2}=p_{r}^{2}+L^{2}/r^{2}\to L^{2}/r^{2}
. Subjecting our aligned-spins Hamiltonian to this condition then gives rise to:

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu^{2}\tilde{S}_{1}^{4}}{\tilde{r}^{6}}\left[C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}\left(\frac{3}{2}-\frac{3\nu}{2}-\frac{3\nu}{4}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(-\frac{3\nu^{2}}{2}+\frac{9\nu}{2}-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{4}-\frac{3\nu^{2}}{2}\right)\right)\right)\right.

(81)

\displaystyle+C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}^{2}\left(-\frac{\nu}{8}+\frac{1}{q}\left(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\nu}{8}\right)\right)+C_{1(\text{BS}^{3})}\left(-\frac{\nu}{2}-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{2}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(-\frac{\nu^{2}}{2}-\frac{5\nu}{2}+\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu^{2}}{2}-\frac{9\nu}{2}+\frac{3}{2}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle+C_{1(\text{ES}^{4})}\left(\frac{5\nu}{2}-\frac{23}{8}+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{16}+\frac{9}{16}\right)+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\frac{5\nu^{2}}{2}-\frac{33\nu}{4}+\frac{23}{8}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu^{2}}{8}+\frac{15\nu}{16}-\frac{9}{16}\right)\right)\right)+\frac{27}{35}C_{1(\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4})}\left(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\nu}{8}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(-\frac{\nu^{2}}{8}+\frac{3\nu}{8}-\frac{1}{8}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu^{3}\tilde{S}_{1}^{3}\tilde{S}_{2}}{\tilde{r}^{6}}\left[3+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(3\nu-3+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{2}\right)\right)+C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}\left(\frac{5}{2}-\frac{9}{2}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\frac{5\nu}{2}+6\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{9}{2}-6\nu\right)\right)\right)+C_{1(\text{BS}^{3})}\left(-6+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\frac{23}{2}-6\nu+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{3\nu}{4}-\frac{13}{4}\right)\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu^{2}\tilde{S}_{1}^{2}\tilde{S}_{2}^{2}}{2\tilde{r}^{6}}\left[3-3\nu\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}+2C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}\left(4\nu+3+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(\frac{9}{2}-\frac{15\nu}{4}\right)+\frac{\nu}{q}\left(4-\frac{9}{2}\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\right)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+C_{1(\text{ES}^{2})}C_{2(\text{ES}^{2})}\left(9+\frac{\tilde{L}^{2}}{\tilde{r}}\left(-\frac{9\nu}{8}-\frac{33}{8}\right)\right)\right]+(1\leftrightarrow 2).

5.1
Gravitational-Wave Observables

Now we can define the binding energies associated with the simplified Hamiltonians above as

e\equiv\tilde{H}
, and relate them to the emitted frequencies of GWs measured in the LIGO, Virgo, or
KAGRA experiments. To that end, we need to supplement the circular-orbit condition of constant
coordinate-separation with Hamilton’s equation,

\dot{p}_{r}=-\partial\tilde{H}(\tilde{r},\tilde{L})/\partial\tilde{r}=0
, which then removes completely
the coordinate dependence from the simplified Hamiltonian in eq. (
81
), and relates, to begin
with, the binding energy with the angular momentum:

\displaystyle(e)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}(\tilde{L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu^{2}\tilde{S}_{1}^{4}}{\tilde{L}^{12}}\left[\left(\frac{285\nu}{4}+\frac{483}{8}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{85\nu}{16}+\frac{417}{16}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}\right.

(82)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{17\nu}{2}+\frac{39}{2}\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}+\left(\frac{43\nu}{16}+\frac{83}{16}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}+\left(\frac{1}{8}-\frac{\nu}{8}\right)\frac{27}{35}C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\left(\frac{423\nu^{2}}{8}+\frac{99\nu}{2}-\frac{483}{8}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{11\nu^{2}}{2}+\frac{749\nu}{16}-\frac{417}{16}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{17\nu^{2}}{2}+\frac{61\nu}{2}-\frac{39}{2}\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}+\left(\frac{23\nu^{2}}{8}+\frac{123\nu}{16}-\frac{83}{16}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\left(-\frac{\nu^{2}}{8}+\frac{3\nu}{8}-\frac{1}{8}\right)\frac{27}{35}C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}\right)\right]+\frac{\nu^{3}\tilde{S}_{1}^{3}\tilde{S}_{2}}{\tilde{L}^{12}}\left[-\frac{717}{4}-35C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+3C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(-\frac{285\nu}{2}-\frac{483}{4}+\left(-\frac{145\nu}{2}-\frac{1455}{4}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{9\nu}{4}-\frac{207}{4}\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu^{2}\tilde{S}_{1}^{2}\tilde{S}_{2}^{2}}{2\tilde{L}^{12}}\left[-\frac{297\nu}{4}-\frac{2025}{4}+\left(-\frac{383\nu}{2}-\frac{951}{4}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(-\frac{3\nu}{2}-\frac{195}{2}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}C_{2\text{ES}^{2}}-\frac{913}{4}\frac{\nu}{q}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\right]+(1\leftrightarrow 2).

To relate to the gauge-invariant frequency, we invoke Hamilton’s equation for the orbital phase,

d\phi/d\tilde{t}\equiv\tilde{\omega}=\partial\tilde{H}(\tilde{r},\tilde{L})/\partial\tilde{L}=0
, and define the PN parameter,
x\equiv\tilde{\omega}^{2/3}
. From this we get the angular momentum as a function of GW frequency:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\nu^{2}x^{6}\tilde{S}_{1}^{4}\left[\left(30\nu+\frac{381}{2}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{99}{4}-\frac{125\nu}{4}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}+\left(-7\nu-69\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\right.

(83)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{61\nu}{4}-\frac{59}{4}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}+\left(1-\nu\right)\frac{27}{35}C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\left(\frac{61\nu^{2}}{2}+351\nu-\frac{381}{2}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left(-\frac{47\nu^{2}}{2}+\frac{323\nu}{4}-\frac{99}{4}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}+\left(-7\nu^{2}-131\nu+69\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\left(\frac{21\nu^{2}}{2}-\frac{179\nu}{4}+\frac{59}{4}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}+\left(-\nu^{2}+3\nu-1\right)\frac{27}{35}C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\nu^{3}x^{6}\tilde{S}_{1}^{3}\tilde{S}_{2}\left[-59+105C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}-51C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\phantom{\frac{1}{\nu q}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(-60\nu-381+\left(75\nu-255\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(111-32\nu\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\nu^{2}x^{6}\tilde{S}_{1}^{2}\tilde{S}_{2}^{2}\left[-29\nu-825+\left(33-2\nu\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(13\nu+5\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}C_{2\text{ES}^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{\nu}{q}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\right]+(1\leftrightarrow 2).

Finally, we can express the binding energy as a function of the GW frequency by combining the former two
relations:

\displaystyle(e)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nu^{2}x^{6}\tilde{S}_{1}^{4}\left[\left(\frac{9\nu}{2}-9\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{135\nu}{16}-\frac{45}{16}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}+\left(9-3\nu\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\right.

(84)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{21}{16}-\frac{99\nu}{16}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}+\left(\frac{3\nu}{8}-\frac{3}{8}\right)\frac{27}{35}C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(\left(6\nu^{2}-\frac{45\nu}{2}+9\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{33\nu^{2}}{4}-\frac{225\nu}{16}+\frac{45}{16}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}+\left(-3\nu^{2}+21\nu-9\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\left(-\frac{39\nu^{2}}{8}+\frac{141\nu}{16}-\frac{21}{16}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}+\left(\frac{3\nu^{2}}{8}-\frac{9\nu}{8}+\frac{3}{8}\right)\frac{27}{35}C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\nu^{3}x^{6}\tilde{S}_{1}^{3}\tilde{S}_{2}\left[-6-\frac{21}{2}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\frac{27}{2}C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{\nu q}\left(18-9\nu+\left(-\frac{27\nu}{4}-\frac{45}{4}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{33\nu}{4}-\frac{9}{4}\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\nu^{2}x^{6}\tilde{S}_{1}^{2}\tilde{S}_{2}^{2}\left[\frac{27\nu}{4}+\frac{81}{4}+\left(3\nu-36\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{33}{2}-\frac{15\nu}{2}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}C_{2\text{ES}^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+6\frac{\nu}{q}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\right]+(1\leftrightarrow 2).

5.2
Extension to Scattering Angles

In a weak gravitational field
2
-to-
2
scattering events can be studied in a perturbative expansion
in
G
, within the so-called post-Minkowskian (PM) approximation.
In this setup the scattering angle for aligned spins in the COM frame can be derived, and only in low
loop orders, as we explained in section
1
, it can then be linked to an extrapolated quantity
associated with the NR binary inspiral in some overlap with the PN approximation. At this point it
should be highlighted that
recently a unique novel approach was put forward in
Edison:2022cdu
, which bypasses the need to
link between scattering and inspiral, and is applicable to any loop order. This approach is formulated
directly in the bound problem, and uses amplitudes methods to reach unprecedented
state-of-the-art results in PN theory
Edison:2022cdu
.

In the present sectors, we can extend our PN aligned-spins Hamiltonian in eq. (
5
) to
a scattering energy function, and using general integration considerations
Damour:1988mr
, we can
then compute here such extrapolated scattering angles as we detailed in
Kim:2022bwv
. We then only
need to truncate the expansion in
G
according to the loop order in the PM approximation, which in
this case is only one loop. Using identical notation to
Kim:2022bwv
:

\displaystyle p_{\infty}=\frac{m_{1}m_{2}}{E}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1},\quad E=\sqrt{m_{1}^{2}+m_{2}^{2}+2m_{1}m_{2}\gamma},\quad\gamma=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-v_{\infty}^{2}}},

(85)

and:

\displaystyle\tilde{b}=\frac{v_{\infty}^{2}}{Gm}b,\qquad\tilde{v}=\frac{v_{\infty}}{c},\qquad\tilde{a}_{i}=\frac{S_{i}}{bm_{i}c},\qquad\Gamma=\frac{E}{mc^{2}}=\sqrt{1+2\nu(\gamma-1)},

(86)

we then write the scattering angles in the NLO quartic-in-spin sectors as:

\displaystyle\theta_{\text{S}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\theta_{\text{S}_{1}^{4}}+\theta_{\text{S}_{1}^{3}\text{S}_{2}}+\theta_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}\text{S}_{2}^{2}}+(1\leftrightarrow 2),

(87)

with

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{\text{S}_{1}^{4}}}{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{1}^{4}\left[\frac{1}{\tilde{b}}C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}(2+2\tilde{v}^{2})+\frac{\pi}{\tilde{b}^{2}}\left(-\frac{75\nu}{16}\tilde{v}^{2}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{15}{16}+\left(\frac{105\nu}{64}+\frac{105}{32}\right)\tilde{v}^{2}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}\right.\right.

\displaystyle+\left(15-\frac{45\nu}{8}\right)\tilde{v}^{2}C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}+\left(\frac{45}{16}+\left(\frac{315}{32}-\frac{75\nu}{16}\right)\tilde{v}^{2}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}+\frac{81}{448}\nu\tilde{v}^{2}C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{\nu}{q}\tilde{v}^{2}\left(-\frac{75}{16}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\frac{105}{64}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}^{2}-\frac{45}{8}C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}-\frac{75}{16}C_{1\text{ES}^{4}}+\frac{81}{448}C_{1\text{E}^{2}\text{S}^{4}}\right)\right)\right],

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{\text{S}_{1}^{3}\text{S}_{2}}}{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{1}^{3}\tilde{a}_{2}\left[\frac{1}{\tilde{b}}C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\left(8+8\tilde{v}^{2}\right)+\frac{\pi}{\tilde{b}^{2}}\left(-\frac{75\nu}{8}\tilde{v}^{2}+\left(\frac{15}{4}+\left(\frac{885}{16}-\frac{195\nu}{16}\right)\tilde{v}^{2}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\right.\right.

(89)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{45}{4}+\left(\frac{735}{16}-\frac{75\nu}{16}\right)\tilde{v}^{2}\right)C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{\nu}{q}\tilde{v}^{2}\left(-\frac{75}{8}-\frac{195}{16}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}-\frac{75}{16}C_{1\text{BS}^{3}}\right)\right)\right],

\displaystyle\frac{\theta_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}\text{S}_{2}^{2}}}{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{a}_{1}^{2}\tilde{a}_{2}^{2}\left[\frac{1}{\tilde{b}}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}C_{2\text{ES}^{2}}\left(12+12\tilde{v}^{2}\right)+\frac{\pi}{\tilde{b}^{2}}\left(\frac{15}{4}+\frac{165}{16}\tilde{v}^{2}\right.\right.

(90)

\displaystyle+2\left(\frac{315\nu}{16}+\frac{375}{16}\right)\tilde{v}^{2}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}+\left(\frac{75}{4}+\frac{225}{4}\tilde{v}^{2}\right)C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}C_{2\text{ES}^{2}}

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{315}{8}\frac{\nu}{q}\tilde{v}^{2}C_{1\text{ES}^{2}}\right)\right].

When all leading spin-induced multipolar Wilson coefficients are set to unity, as stipulated in

Levi:2015msa
for BHs, namely
C_{\text{ES}^{2}}=C_{\text{BS}^{3}}=C_{\text{ES}^{4}}=1
, and
depending on our conjecture
2
in section
2
, and the related
matching of the new Wilson coefficient
C_{\text{E${}^{2}$S${}^{4}$}}
for BHs from full GR or real-world
data, then our scattering angles may agree or not with those presented for BHs in

Guevara:2018wpp
.
In any case, as noted in
Levi:2022dqm
and in section
2
, the derivations
and results in Guevara et al.
Guevara:2018wpp
are inherently dependent on our free-standing
framework, as they relied on our worldline spin theory and results introduced in

Levi:2014gsa
;
Levi:2015msa
, which was omitted in
Guevara:2018wpp
.

At this point it is important to reiterate that such limited observables provide but a very partial
physical picture of the system, and even more so for higher-spin sectors, where the full information is
found at the most general Hamiltonians first presented in section
3.2
above. This further
highlights the indispensable comprehensive confirmation of the general Hamiltonians provided through
the Poincaré algebra that we found in section
4
above.

6
Conclusions

We put forward a broader picture of the effective theory of a spinning particle, and in
particular of Kerr BHs, within the EFT of spinning gravitating objects
Levi:2015msa
. We also
fully derived and established the new precision frontier at the
5
PN order for GW measurements from
inspirals and mergers of generic compact binaries. The
5
PN precision frontier includes higher-spin
sectors, quadratic and quartic in the spin, which both display novel physical effects, originating from
the extension of the effective theory beyond linear order in the curvature. In the third subleading
quadratic-in-spin sectors there is a new tidal effect, and in the quartic-in-spin sectors there is a
new spin-induced multipolar effect. Using these observations, and with eyes towards the next precision
frontier at the
6
PN order, we generalized the concept of tidal and of spin-induced
multipolar operators, and made conjectures on the numerical values of their Wilson coefficients for
rotating BHs in GR in
4
spacetime dimensions.

We then confirmed the generalized actions for generic
compact binaries of the complete NLO quartic-in-spin sectors, which were computed via the extension of
the EFT of gravitating spinning objects in
Levi:2020lfn
.
We derived for the first time the NLO quartic-in spin interaction potentials, that consist of no less
than
12
distinct sectors, with a new one due to the new spin-induced multipolar operator
that is quadratic in the curvature. We also derived for the first time the corresponding general
Hamiltonians in an arbitrary reference frame. These Hamiltonians give the full physical information on
the binary system, which mostly gets lost in higher-spin sectors, when going to common observables
which assume an aligned-spins configuration. This is since generic spin orientations of the rotating
components of the binary have an observational signature in the gravitational waveforms.

Moreover, the general Hamiltonians obtained exclusively via our framework uniquely enable to find the
global Poincaré algebra, which we carried out successfully in all the sectors with spins that
contribute at the present
5
PN order. It should be noted that in order to accomplish that, we needed
to significantly scale our approach to the solution of the Poincaré algebra. Thus we solved
for the full Poincaré algebra of the third subleading quadratic-in-spin Hamiltonians first
presented in
Kim:2022bwv
, and of the NLO quartic-in-spin sectors computed in

Levi:2020lfn
. This solution of the Poincaré algebra also provides the most stringent
consistency check to the validity of our new comprehensive state-of-the-art results.

We proceeded to derive simplified Hamiltonians in restricted kinematic configurations, starting with
the COM Hamiltonians. We then derived observables for GW applications, namely gauge-invariant
relations among binding energies, angular momentum, and GW frequency.
Furthermore, to make contact with the scattering problem, we also derived the extrapolated scattering
angles, relevant only in the aligned-spins configuration. We could then specify our consequent
scattering angles to the scattering of BHs, by fixing all leading spin-induced multipolar Wilson
coefficients to
1
, as we prescribed in
Levi:2015msa
; and depending on our conjecture

2
in section
2
, and the related matching of the new Wilson
coefficient in the present NLO quartic-in-spin sectors,
C_{\text{E${}^{2}$S${}^{4}$}}
, for BHs from full GR
or real-world data.
In any case, as noted in
Levi:2022dqm
and in section
2
, the derivations
and results in the few overlapping scattering-amplitudes studies are inherently dependent on our
free-standing framework, as they relied on our worldline spin theory and prior results introduced in

Levi:2014gsa
;
Levi:2015msa
. We reiterate however, that such limited scattering observables can
provide but very partial physical input on the system, especially at higher-spin sectors. The solution
of the Poincaré algebra on the other hand provides the strongest confirmation that the
5
PN order
has now been established as the new precision frontier.

Redefinition of Rotational Variables

As noted in section
3.1
, the new redefinitions of the spin in the present sectors are
fixed as:

\displaystyle\left(\omega^{ij}_{1}\right)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}^{4}}=\left(\omega^{ij}_{1}\right)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{4}}+\left(\omega^{ij}_{1}\right)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{3}\text{S}_{2}}+\left(\omega^{ij}_{1}\right)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{2}\text{S}_{2}^{2}}+\left(\omega^{ij}_{1}\right)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}\text{S}_{2}^{3}}-(i\leftrightarrow j),

(91)

where

\displaystyle\left(\omega^{ij}_{1}\right)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{GC_{1\text{BS}^{3}}m_{2}}{4m_{1}{}^{3}r{}^{4}}\Big[20S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}+2S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ki}v_{1}^{k}n^{j}-2S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{kj}n^{k}v_{1}^{i}

(92)

\displaystyle+8S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ij}\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}-10S_{1}^{ki}v_{1}^{k}n^{j}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+5S_{1}^{ij}\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}

\displaystyle-30\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}n^{j}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}-13\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}S_{1}^{ij}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{3GC_{1\text{ES}^{2}}m_{2}}{8m_{1}{}^{3}r{}^{4}}\Big[20S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}+5S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ki}v_{1}^{k}n^{j}-S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{kj}n^{k}v_{1}^{i}

\displaystyle+9S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ij}\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}-15S_{1}^{ki}v_{1}^{k}n^{j}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+5S_{1}^{kj}n^{k}v_{1}^{i}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}

\displaystyle-20\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}n^{j}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}-9\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}S_{1}^{ij}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{GC_{1\text{ES}^{4}}m_{2}}{8m_{1}{}^{3}r{}^{4}}\Big[10S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}\big(\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}-4\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}\big)-2S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ki}v_{1}^{k}n^{j}

\displaystyle-4S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ki}v_{2}^{k}n^{j}+2S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{kj}n^{k}\big(v_{1}^{i}+2v_{2}^{i}\big)+S_{1}^{2}S_{1}^{ij}\big(\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}-19\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\big)

\displaystyle+70S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+10S_{1}^{ki}v_{2}^{k}n^{j}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+5S_{1}^{ij}\big(\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}

\displaystyle+4\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\big)(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+30\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}n^{j}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}-2\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}S_{1}^{ij}

\displaystyle+11\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}S_{1}^{ij}\Big],

\displaystyle\left(\omega^{ij}_{1}\right)^{\text{NLO}}_{\text{S}_{1}^{3}\text{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{GC_{1\text{BS}^{3}}}{8m_{1}{}^{2}r{}^{4}}\Big[20\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}\big(4\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}-3\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}\big)-10S_{1}^{2}S_{2}^{ki}n^{k}\big(2\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}

(93)

\displaystyle-5\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}\big)-4\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}S_{1}^{ki}S_{2}^{k}n^{j}-16\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}S_{1}^{ki}v_{1}^{k}n^{j}+4S_{1}^{2}S_{2}^{ki}v_{1}^{k}n^{j}

\displaystyle+24\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}S_{1}^{ki}v_{2}^{k}n^{j}-10S_{1}^{2}S_{2}^{ki}v_{2}^{k}n^{j}+4\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}S_{1}^{kj}n^{k}\big(4v_{1}^{i}-5v_{2}^{i}\big)

\displaystyle-2S_{1}^{2}S_{2}^{kj}n^{k}\big(14v_{1}^{i}-11v_{2}^{i}\big)+4S_{1}^{i}S_{1}^{kj}S_{2}^{k}\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}+4\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}S_{1}^{ij}\big(5\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}-2\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\big)

\displaystyle+4S_{1}^{2}S_{2}^{ij}\big(\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}+3\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\big)-210S_{2}^{ki}n^{k}\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}+20S_{2}^{ki}v_{2}^{k}n^{j}(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}

\displaystyle+10S_{2}^{ij}\big(2\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}-11\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\big)(\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n})^{2}-120\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}n^{j}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}

\displaystyle+40\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}n^{j}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}+90\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}n^{j}S_{1}^{ki}n^{k}-20\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}n^{j}S_{2}^{ki}n^{k}

\displaystyle-10\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}n^{j}S_{1}^{ki}v_{2}^{k}-4\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{i}S_{1}^{kj}v_{2}^{k}+4\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}S_{1}^{i}S_{2}^{kj}v_{2}^{k}

\displaystyle-5\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\big(4\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}-9\vec{v}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\big)S_{1}^{ij}+4\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}S_{1}^{ij}-32\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}S_{1}^{ij}

\displaystyle+14\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}S_{1}^{ij}+23\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{2}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}S_{1}^{ij}-8\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{n}\vec{S}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{1}S_{2}^{ij}\Big]
